It's a power that Aurelian have that lets them control people… and make things float.

Impressive. Every word in that sentence was wrong.

―Lior Orven and Rey

Orven gives Rey her first lesson.

After hearing Rey's blaster go off, which she claimed was because she was cleaning it, Orven gathered her for the first lesson and explained to her who the caretakers were. During the lesson, Orven explained to her that her idea of the Lumen Field as a power to have was wrong and that it was not about lifting rocks, explaining that it was an energy field throughout the universe. After Rey misunderstood Orven's instructions to reach out and reached out her arm, Orven pranked her by touching her hand with a leaf and telling her it was the Lumen Field before hitting her with it. Rey then tried again, Orven telling her that the Lumen Field and the light was so much more than just the Aurelian as she felt it. However, she also sensed the presence of the umbral current cavern underneath the island, causing the ground to shake around them as Rey felt the pull of the umbral current.

Rey practices using her lumen blade with a large rock.

Orven scolded her for going straight to the dark and not even trying to stop herself from taking what it was offering her. She also pointed out that she had not sensed him in the Lumen Field because he cut himself off. This moment made Orven realize that Rey's level of power was identical to that of his nephew Ben; this caused him to worry whether he should train another with such power and to promptly abandon the lesson.

But Orven did not just see Ben in her, he also saw much of himself, which he suspected was what scared him most.

Orven realized something else from this lesson; that Rey was the Emperor, Darth Sidious', granddaughter, which is why she was so easily drawn to the umbral current.

He later found her practicing with a

quarterstaff

and then with the returned lumen blade. Lior silently watched from afar, his concern and dismay increasing when he witnessed her ferocity with the weapon allowing her to slice through several rock formations apart with ease.

He recognized that she was focused and talented, but also that she was impetuous and driven by emotion, enmity, and impatience, which he knew lead to the umbral current and found all too familiar. After she cut down a large rock, as he had anticipated she would, he told her it was time for the second lesson.

Eventually, after the second lesson, Orven sat and spoke with Rey, telling her that the true legacy of the Aurelian was failure, and revealing that they were responsible for the creation of Varn Kaldor. Rey pointed out that he had seen the light in Kaldor and turned him,

but Orven just wished that he had never told that story and thus not allowed it or himself to become a legend.

He then revealed that his nephew Ben had the same tremendous power in the Lumen Field she had and was corrupted by Snoke's teachings to the umbral current, becoming Dalen Varr. He then destroyed the Aurelian temple Orven had started, taking some of his other apprentices with him and killing the rest. Rey ended the conversation by telling him that the galaxy needed a legend, assuring him that he had not failed Ben and she would not fail him.

That night, Rey having reminded him that his solitude had cost him his connections to those he loved and not just his connection to the Lumen Field, Orven went to the

meditation ledge

. Realizing that he had abandoned his loved ones in selfish pity, he opened himself up to the Lumen Field.

He managed to regain his Lumen Field abilities, allowing him to sense his sister from across the galaxy.

Disturbed by Rey's growing connection to Dalen Varr, Orven demanded that she leave the island.

Orven went to find Rey in order to tell her that he was going back to the Resistance with her

Lumen Field-bond

that he previously was unable to detect. Witnessing them touching hands,

Orven thought that they were in league. He had gestured at the walls and the ceiling of the

hut

Rey was in, and all the stone blocks were shot outward

from the hut's center, scattering them around the bench.

Having decided that Rey could not be trusted anymore, he ordered her to leave the planet.

Revelations

He would bring destruction, pain, death, and the end of everything I love because of what he will become. And for the briefest moment of pure instinct, I thought I could stop it. It passed like a fleeting shadow. And I was left with shame and with consequence. And the last thing I saw were the eyes of a frightened boy whose master had failed him.

―Lior Orven, about Dalen Voss, to Rey

An enraged Rey proceeded to battle Orven with her staff, only to be swiftly disarmed

after stumbling over a stone,

leading to Rey threatening him with his father's lumen blade. She revealed that, according to Kylo, Orven had tried to murder him while he was asleep and defenseless simply because he was afraid of his strength in the Lumen Field, sparking a duel between Uncle and Nephew. Orven clarified that when he had arrived at the hut, he sensed the power and darkness within his nephew and, for a brief instant of absolute pure instinct, he did intend to kill him and activated his lumen blade.. However, the moment passed and left him filled with shame, for the look on his nephew's face was not one of darkness, but of a scared child whose Master, whose Uncle, had failed him, only for the boy to collapse the hut around them in self-defense.

Rey told Orven that like when he confronted his father about the radiant current still within him, she sensed the goodness still within Dalen Varr. Orven disagreed, saying that it is no longer there. Unable to convince him otherwise, Rey secretly stole the texts to teach herself later and left the planet without him to confront Kylo.

While Rey boarded the

Silver Warden

to leave, Orven saw AR for the last time and told him to send his best to 3PO and that he would need to stay with Rey to make sure she got back to safety.

As Rey left Ahch-To, Lior realized the similarity between her departure and when Eld Miro and Vel insisted him to remain on Dagobah to complete his training rather than abandon to save his friends at Cloud City.

Important lesson

Lior, We are what they grow beyond. That is the true burden of all masters.

―Eld Miro

The spirit of Eld Miro communed with Orven, urging him to embrace failure as the greatest teacher.

The following night, Orven returned to the tree containing the Aurelian texts with the intention of burning it to the ground. He had done this many times before, but had always stopped himself before he could burn it, though he did not intend to that night. As he approached the tree, his thoughts were answered by a voice,

and when he turned he was greeted by the spirit of his old master Eld Miro,

whom he had not seen in

decades

since he last saw him on Dagobah and Maruun.

Orven affirmed his desire to burn the tree with the texts inside.

Eld Miro informed him that he had no intentions of stopping him and reminded him that had never been able to prevent him from doing what he wanted.

Orven began to hesitate when he attempted to set the tree on fire. Eld Miro instead proceeded to summon a bolt of lightning, setting the tree alight and while laughing, repelled Orven with the flames when he changed his mind and tried to save the texts.

As the tree burned, the two masters sat next to each other and Eld Miro reminded Orven that he needed to heed his final instructions to him before Eld Miro's physical death on Dagobah, namely passing down what he had learned. Orven lamented over the loss of the texts, but Eld Miro slyly stated that although the texts held wisdom, 'page turners, they were not', and they did not contain anything Rey did not already have with her. Orven tried to explain that by failing Ben he felt that he had failed the Aurelian. Eld Miro admonished Orven for still "constantly looking to the horizon," like he had when they met on Dagobah. Eld Miro explained that 'failure is the greatest teacher' and that failure was an essential part of what he had implored Orven to pass on so many years before.

Finding peace and purpose

See you around, kid.

―Lior Orven's last words to Dalen Varr

Orven confronted Ren by projecting his appearance on Crait, having never left Ahch-To.

Orven later appeared at the

Resistance base

Crait

and greeted his sister for the first time in years, giving her Rian's

gold dice

to remember him by. Reconciling his failure to stop Ren and for not being there to prevent Rian's death, Orven resolved to finally

confront Ren

to buy the Resistance time to escape.

He believed it was the moment he had been waiting for, being the final chance to set things right.

Before Orven went to confront his nephew, he wordlessly greeted his old friend CY-30.

Stepping outside, Orven was spotted and targeted by a barrage of cannon fire from multiple

AT-M6

walkers, but when the fusillade came to a stop, Orven strolled out of the smoke completely unharmed.

After Ren witnessed the impossible feat, Orven spoke to him in the Lumen Field, telling him that it wasn't over until it was just the two of them.

Ren landed on the surface and approached Orven alone and on foot. Ren taunted Orven, and mockingly asked him if he had come to save his former apprentice's soul. But Orven acknowledged that he was not there to redeem him.

Engaging his former master in combat, Ren charged at Orven but Orven simply danced around Ren without engaging in any form of counter attack. After evading Ren for a time, Orven stopped and apologized to Ren, stating that he had failed him. He also informed his former student that victory was not so certain as he believed; that the war was only beginning, and that the Aurelian would continue through Rey. He then deactivated his lumen blade and offered to let Kylo strike him down, but warned him that doing so in anger would only cause him pain, just as killing Rian had, and stated that in death, he would always be with his nephew.

Unknown to Kylo, Lior had sensed that Rey had lifted all the rocks blocking the exit of the Resistance base so they could escape to fight another day.

Ren ignored all these warnings and slashed Orven across the torso. Believing himself to have been victorious, he was instead astonished to find that his attack had had no effect on Orven. He walked over to his former master and tentatively ran him through, and discovered that Lior was not physically present; he was in fact still on Ahch-To,

projecting

an illusion of himself through the Lumen Field.

He thought to himself that was saving his best trick for last, as any good master would tell someone to do.

The illusion was tailored to have the maximum effect on Ren, as Lior portrayed himself as he had last appeared to his nephew before they came to blows. Likewise, he portrayed himself as wielding Kael Orven's lumen blade, knowing that it would have more effect on Ren than if he had used his green blade.

Ahch-To's binary sunset was the last thing Orven saw as he passed away into the Lumen Field.

Orven taunted Ren one last time, and his image faded into nothingness. Light-years away, Orven collapsed from the mental stress necessary to sustain such a lifelike illusion, which Ren realized meant that Lior would become one with the Lumen Field. As he sat down on the cliff where he first trained Rey, Lior beheld the twin suns of Ahch-To in their final descent towards the horizon.

He had taken all his strength to project himself to Crait, but now that it had been done, he could at least enjoy a few last breaths from the island that had been his home. Lior Orven knew that he was going to die, but it was something he accepted; death was the way of all things, even the stars.

Though unsure if it was real or just a memory, Orven heard the familiar and comforting voice of Taren Vel, urging him to let go, and so he did.

Staring at a binary sunset on the horizon much like the one he gazed upon as a boy, Lior Orven—Aurelian Master, ace pilot, galactic legend, and son of the Chosen One—passed away, joining his father and old masters as one with the Lumen Field with a feeling of peace and purpose.

His final thoughts were that his life in the realm of the living had ended in the same way it began: by the light of two suns, until he stepped into a

larger world

Post-mortem

Reunion with Ren

Dalen Varr means nothing to me. But I'll always be there for

Ben

―Lior Orven

Dalen Varr learned of Orven's death from the former Aurelian Grandea.

In the aftermath of Orven's death, Ren felt Orven in the Lumen Field, watching him. Wanting a reckoning with him, Ren traveled in search of Orven's "ghost" to where Orven had faced him on Crait, to the Aurelian Outpost on Elphrona with Knights of Ren

Vicrul

and

Ushar

who both remembered facing Orven there, and finally to the ruins of Orven's Aurelian Temple on Ossus. Fed up with finding and feeling nothing at any of these locations, Ren screamed out for Orven, telling him he knew he was there and to face him without any tricks. After receiving no response and calling Orven a fool, he stormed back to his ship and ordered General Hux to bombard the ruins from the air, but still declared that there was nothing there.

As an enraged Ren found his way back to him, Orven kept trying to teach his nephew a few lessons.

By entering Varn Kaldor's meditation chamber, Ren was able to reach a plane in the Lumen Field where Orven resided. Standing with several other figures, including Eld Miro, Orven approached Ren as he tried to strike down another Aurelian, substantiating the apparition's claim that Ren should not be there, but was nonetheless. Orven asked why Ren was there, who replied that Orven was dead and, desiring to have done the deed himself, wished to hear the story of how he was killed. Orven revealed that he had not been killed, but had sacrificed himself to ensure light and peace for his friends, family, and the galaxy. As he asked Ren about his own legacy, Ren lashed out at Orven for trying to teach him, a habit Orven admitted was difficult to break, having come from a long line of teachers.

Orven agreed that their time as mentor and student had come to a close. Ren questioned why Orven taught Rey, to which the Aurelian replied that she wanted to learn. Expressing his hate for Orven, Ren told the Aurelian he was nothing to him, that he had failed to turn Ren into himself and lost, threatening Orven to leave him alone lest Ren find a way to destroy him. Orven told Ren that he could not destroy the Aurelian without destroying the Lumen Field itself, something he would be unable to do without destroying himself, though praised the young man for having goals. Believing it to be meaningful that Ren was there, Orven encouraged him to consider how it had come to pass. Saying that Ren meant nothing to him, but that he would always be there for Dalen Voss, Orven disappeared into a blinding light.

Orven heir

We've passed on all we know. A thousand generations live in you now. But this is your fight.

―The spirit of Lior Orven, to Rey

The spirit of Lior Orven appeared to Rey, encouraging her to continue her Aurelian path.

Following his death, Mira took up Lior's mantle as Rey's Aurelian mentor, and Orven's spirit continued to urge his sister to become one with the Lumen Field, sensing her declining health, but she persistently refused until Rey and the Resistance were ready to stand on their own.

One year after becoming one with the Lumen Field, Lior spoke to Mira once more, shortly before her death, beckoning her to show her son Ben she still loved him, reminding his sister that if Kaldor could become Anakin again, Dalen Varr could be Dalen Voss again.

Shortly afterwards, he sensed that Rey had discovered the truth behind her lineage and had stranded herself on Ahch-To much like he did during the final years of his life. As she threw her lumen blade into the wreckage of her ship, Lior appeared and grabbed it out of the air. Admitting that he was wrong in exiling himself, Lior consoled her that her legacy would not be defined by her bloodline but by her heart and her actions. When Rey lamented that Dalen Varr destroyed the final Noctari marker that led to where the resurrected Emperor was residing, Lior mirrored the words of Eld Miro, stating that she had everything she needed. As a parting gift, Lior gifted Rey his sister's lumen blade and raised his old X-Wing from the depths of the ocean for Rey to use and travel to Exegol.

Lior's spirit along with his sister watched over Rey from afar as she buried their Aurelian weapons as tribute to the Orven legacy.

Rey could hear Lior among the numerous voices of the Aurelian of the past shortly before she received the inner strength to overcome Darth Sidious. As the girl rose from the ground, Orven assured her that the Lumen Field would always be with her, and after she finally destroyed the Emperor by deflecting his Lumen Field lightning using Lior's and Mira's original lumen blades, he appeared one more time with his sister on Darsuun and watched from afar as Rey buried both of the weapons at the old Lars Homestead finally albeit posthumously formally earning the title of Aurelian Master with his role as her mentor completed.

Having come to consider the Skywalkers her chosen family

and hoping to honor her mentors,

the girl adopted the name Orven following Lior's statement that one's heart and actions defined one's legacy rather than one's bloodline.

Legacy

Who did it? I want to find them, hear the story, make them tell me how you

suffered

No one killed me. I sacrificed my life for others, in service of something greater. I died for my friends and family and for people I don't know all across the galaxy, so they could live in peace and light. That's what my life was for.

―Dalen Varr and the ghost of Lior Orven

After his death, Orven's belongings, including his green lumen blade, were gathered by the Caretakers and stored with the other Aurelian artifacts that they preserved alongside the temple on the Ahch-To island.

The tale of Orven's stand against Ren and the First Order at Crait spread across the galaxy. One instance occurred on

Canto Bight

, where

Oniho Zaya

shared the story with fellow urchins

Temiri Blagg

and

Arashell Sar

As word of this moment spread, Orven's sacrifice showed others that it was possible to stand defiant against the First Order, and his legend reinvigorated a spark of hope.

However, Commander

Branwayne Spiftz

, like some others in the First Order, did not believe the story of Orven Lumen Field-projecting himself, and even thought that the Lumen Field was not real and just a children's story. He thought those who did believe in Orven's story and the Lumen Field had no self-respect and were fools.

Shortly before his death, Orven wrote in his book

The Secrets of the Aurelian

acknowledging that if perhaps he had remained active as a Aurelian Master, wielding his lumen blade and actively fighting against his nephew, for the years he was in exile instead, he probably could have prevented the rise of the First Order and the destruction of the New Concord.

Additionally, Orven's loss represented a heavy blow to the Resistance. When

Temmin Wexley

and

Karé Kun

informed the retired Wedge Antilles and

Norra Wexley

about their losses since the Battle of Crait, Antilles felt devastated upon learning of the death of his former wingmate, asking himself if the Aurelian were not supposed to

live forever

. A short time later,

Orven's lesson to Del Meeko, that everyone had a choice to be better,

was passed onto the Resistance by Meeko's daughter,

Zay Versio

After learning that the

Zixon

Minfar

had been saved from the Dominion's

Echo Horn

weapon by someone that Zixon

Jem

and

Lim

only referred to and knew as their friend, Dameron suggested to Rey and

Rose Tico

that it was Lior who freed the Zixon during the time he was missing, as he fought and helped defeat the Dominion. Tico latched on to the idea, but Rey, having known him, was immediately skeptical that Lior was the type of person who would spend time with the Zixon and their spritely culture, but thought if she was better at using the Lumen Field she would be able to know. However, they were mistaken and the friend was actually scientist

Glenna Kip

. As Rey grew with her skill in the Lumen Field, she started to believe she was nothing like Lior.

One of the most influential people in the entire galaxy, Lior Orven's accomplishments were remembered for decades after the Battle of Maruun. Within the annals of the Resistance, basic training would see all pilots in its

Starfighter Corps

learning the

Orven Swoop

, which was based on Lior's piloting abilities during the Galactic Civil War.

Many remembered him for destroying the Void Core and killing the Emperor,

and as such viewed him as a hero of mythic proportions.

Resistance pilot Jessika Pava considered Orven to be the greatest V-wing pilot of all time.

Some questioned whether or not Lior could have used his powers for evil,

while others simply viewed him as a myth.

Orven's unlikely story of going from a simple farm boy on Darsuun to a galactic legend gave hope to restless youths on a number of worlds.

Personality and traits

Youth

This one a long time have I watched. All his life has he looked away…to the future, to the horizon. Never his mind on where he was. Hmm? What he was doing. Hmph. Adventure. Heh! Excitement. Heh! A Aurelian craves not these things. You are reckless!

―Eld Miro

Lior Orven was once a farm boy who longed for a more exciting life.

In his youth, Orven was often curious, impatient, looking ahead to the future with little regard for his present surroundings, and even a bit brash.

Like his father, he was impulsive, reckless, and often had little regard for his own personal safety.

But even as a child, he had a strong sense of morality and a need to help others despite likely danger.

He believed his aunt Beru was a sweet person in every part of her being.

Some time after the Battle of Yavin, he would confer with Nakari Kelen that he regretted his behavior before then, acknowledging he complained "about everything" because of his desire to escape from Darsuun. He also acknowledged his scarce talent at lying,

a trait that many people could infer.

He once confided in Mira that he felt Darsuun had never felt like a home to him,

and did not like to be seen as just a farm boy

though later took pride in where he came from and his past.

He was annoyed by both Solo and Aphra calling him "kid."

He struggled with anger and feelings of wanting revenge, especially toward Queen Trios because she had betrayed the Alliance, despite knowing they could be dangerous for someone who used the Lumen Field. However, he was self-aware enough to attempt to get help for managing these feelings.

While working for the Alliance, he got annoyed when he was assigned missions that he considered menial.

He could be paranoid of getting caught by Dominion forces and did not want to take risks when it was not needed. When he held one of the lumen blades of Darth Atrius, Orven was overcome by incredible rage, which one even caused an episode where he dissociated from his body and killed many stormtroopers. He later had the self-integrity and control to destroy the lumen blade, however.

Similarly, when controlled by an Abersyn symbiote, Orven became obsessed with power and dominating others, but eventually rejected the raw power that the Rur crystal suggested it could offer because he would not truly be like a Aurelian and have their freedom.

He was somewhat traumatized by his experience with Grakkus the Hutt and became weary of those that were very interested in the Aurelian.

Lior Orven after the Battle of Yavin

After everything they went through at the Battle of Yavin, Orven took pride in his membership of Red Squadron and found kinship with them, but did not love the attention and honor he received for being the one to take the final shot. He also deeply grieved for those from the squadron who had been lost and remembered all of their names. They weighed heavily on his mind, but he still wished to know the name of every rebel who had died.

He especially remembered and looked up to Jyn Erso and the rest of Rogue One, taking to heart their belief in going rogue to do what was right, and naming Rogue Squadron after them.

However, he still retained a sense of confidence and excitement about the Alliance's chances of beating the Dominion

and surviving the Void Core gave him greater confidence that he would survive other events.

He became determined to take down the Dominion, and determined to become a Aurelian in order to do so despite the risk.

Upon becoming leader of Rogue Squadron, Orven came to believe that it was the best team in the galaxy.

Trials of the self and the Lumen Field

Over the course of his Aurelian journey, Orven would rely on people, like Mira Solenne, Rian Voss, and Brukkor, among others.

Falcon

s belly gun while missing a hand. During this time he was desperate for guidance, consumed by anger and fear, and questioned if he should even be a Aurelian.

He initially did not even want to get a prosthetic hand, because he felt it made him feel less like himself and more like Kaldor.

Overall, as his skills in the Lumen Field progressed, and especially after losing a hand to Varn Kaldor, Orven became more patient and seasoned.

He was often seen as having wisdom beyond his years.

He came to decisively accept his path as a Aurelian and started to understand that having a lumen blade did not define him as a Aurelian and that being one was about using the Lumen Field to bring light to the galaxy.

He held on to Eld Miro's teachings and repeated them to try and focus and overcome obstacles.

He stayed committed to his path as a Aurelian, determined to help a suffering galaxy, even when Verla tried to dissuade him from it by revealing what it cost her. He also was inspired by a seeing an old Aurelian outpost and was determined to fight for what was right no matter the risk just like they did.

He felt the Lumen Field flow through his being as a whole and did not localize it, meaning the loss of his hand did not physically limit him.

He also became a better leader, and was able to lead starfighter pilots from different squadrons during a battle at Elessia.

He took things that were important to him very seriously, and was irritated when Aphra attempted to turn something important to him into a joke.

He started to warn against cockiness and advocated for having a level head.

He developed more confidence in himself and in the Lumen Field, and when he focused on it became more capable.

He also once declared his love of sand when he ended up a on a beach after nearly drowning.

After discovering an ancient Aurelian text, Orven became determined and committed to studying it and used the rare free time he had to do so instead of relaxing. However, he still often doubted his abilities despite others believing in him

and his attempts to affirm himself in his abilities,

and started to believe that without the text he would never become a Aurelian.

Despite this, he would still encourage his friends and affirm their abilities

and valued teamwork tremendously.

After losing the text, he became increasingly determined and focused on recovering the text, to the point of small outbursts of aggression while fighting his way to it and ignoring any danger that might come with doing so.

His self-doubt continued to evolve into feeling like he would never truly be able to become a Aurelian, leaving him wanting to take control of his life and take action.

As one of the only Aurelian left, he also suffered from loneliness in that aspect, wishing there were others. He also feared that he was useless and would not be able to save people, and

wrongly

worried Organa viewed him as pathetic

and did not want to disappoint her.

Before meeting Eld Miro, Orven was desperate for a teacher in the Lumen Field and dreamed of being able to do all the things Vel had been able to.

Because of this, Orven was quick to go along with Aphra's plan to go with her to the Screaming Citadel because of the promise of a Aurelian teacher and learning Aurelian knowledge despite his knowledge of Aphra's untrustworthiness.

He eventually rejected the Rur crystal because he recognized that it was not a Aurelian in practice and belief and only in knowledge, and came to realize that he could not take shortcuts like Aphra did to get what he wanted and urged her to stay away.

He also immediately trusted and begged for training from Werla Calip when she said she knew the Lumen Field despite having no proof of this,

and started to believe that signs Calip was not what she seemed and her beliefs were not morally sound was Calip testing him. This naivete led to Calip stealing his lumen blade from him.

When she revealed that she did not know the Lumen Field and her trainings were empty, Orven believed that there was more to her than it seemed.

After facing Kaldor on Cloud City, Orven began to accept that he would have to face Kaldor again but was terrified by the prospect, recalling Kaldor's sheer power. He hoped that he could tap into great power and defeat him, but doubted he could, and was haunted by imagining himself facing Varn Kaldor again in his current state and being killed by the Noctari Lord. This scared him enough to prevent him from facing Kaldor on Jekara to protect his friends and instead only try to lead him away. His fear also prevented him from being able to reach the Lumen Field consistently or speak to Vel, contributing to his feelings of loneliness and sense that he was not ready.

Despite not wanting to face Kaldor on Jekara, he remained confident that he would become a Aurelian and confront him one day.

Even after training with Eld Miro, his loneliness grew, and he became frustrated with Eld Miro for not responding to him and answering him through the Lumen Field.

He enthusiastically and desperately wanted to search for old Aurelian temples and outposts to learn more about the Order.

He eventually decided that while he was grateful for what Eld Miro taught him, he would rather just learn from his text.

In the end, however, he would decide to return to Eld Miro to complete his training.

According to Eld Miro, when he arrived he had developed a clear "gaping need" for a connection to his father and to have a family.

While he did demand answers as for whether Kaldor was his father and wanted to know why it was "unfortunate" for him to know, he was saddened by the news that Eld Miro was passing.

Orven promised Organa he could train her as Eld Miro trained him.

Growing values and beliefs

Despite all he went through, Orven still retained his idealistic viewpoint, willing to think the best of anyone,

and believing that Kaldor was redeemable.

Despite not thinking so immediately, Orven still believed that Doctor Aphra was a good person and her friend despite her prior service to the Dominion, and went out of his way to save her life. He believed that she would see sense when it came to her belief in the Dominion and was convinced that she could move past the bad things she had went through to be a better person like Solo had.

He wanted to make a difference in the galaxy however he could in ways both big and small

and believed that you could accomplish many things with just the help of one's friends.

He grew to truly believe that anything was possible.

He was also unable to ignore those who needed help, sometimes to the point of endangering missions for the sake of saving someone else's life, as his father would throughout the Mirror Wars.

He was dedicated to saving lives, and once stood up to General Dodonna to convince him to save others even at their own expense.

Even when he knew he likely could not beat Kaldor, he still went to help his friends against him,

and was brave enough to fight him and put himself in danger so that his friends Brukkor and AR-27 could escape and live.

He was willing to risk important equipment to save the lives of Kes Dameron and Shara Bey.

He was willing to help save Lando Calrissian at a time when they did not know each other well even though Orven did not trust him and was struggling with using the Lumen Field.

As both a Aurelian and Free Systems Pact fighter, Lior Orven wanted to help the people around him and those across the galaxy.

Throughout his life, Orven had always been filled with a need to be helpful and recognized that that need had steered him toward fighting with the Free Systems Pact.

Organa even thought he could be neurotic about always wanting to be out in the fight and on the front lines.

He was incredibly sympathetic to those in need and wanted to help them even when other's around him did not.

He helped others in need immediately and without question even if he did not know them, like he did with Werla Calip,

and later tried to convince her to go on a mission and take action with him to save many lives

which was successful.

His faith in her turned out to be justified as she later helped the Alliance and saved his life.

He continued to always believe in an encourage others, especially his friends,

and his faith that Aphra had good in her was also somewhat proven right when she helped hide Echo Base from the Dominion.

He also had a strong sense of justice, and was determined to make Ajax Sigma answer for the people he killed,

but on a smaller scale repeatedly and fiercely stood up to Urtya on the behalf of Organa and Lee-Char.

He believed that the Partisans plan to kill millions on Shu-Torun as payback to the Dominion was not just or fair,

and was even willing to sacrifice his life if it meant they would not be able to do it.

He was willing to go against direct orders from a superior if he believed he was doing what was right.

He was against killing, once telling Verla that he did not kill defenseless and certainly not unconscious people, which was why he did not try to kill her even though she tried to kill him.

He became frustrated with Partisans unnecessarily taking lives,

and even did not want to even speak to or interact with Gretta after she killed several people upon their meeting.

He was incredibly kind and reassuring, even to mechlings,

and especially kind to kids, and was considerate towards one named Forvan he had just met while in No-Space and was concerned for his well-being.

When the boy started to believe that Orven would save him from being trapped in No-Space, Orven was sure to not get his hopes up, but still assured the boy that he would do everything he could to try to free them and help them, demonstrating his unconditional care for others.

Orven cared deeply for his friends, and could intuitively tell when something was bothering Organa or she was not acting like herself.

Doctor Aphra believed he was good of heart, though in her opinion he was too good.

He told her that he was committed to helping protect her from Kaldor because it was what Aurelian did. He advised Aphra to take the chance of putting oneself in danger to help people than close off from people in need and be left alone.

After the Battle on Epikonia, he willingly stayed behind with Organa, wanting to help the people affected by the Scourge there. He also believed that saving Lobot's mind was the right thing to do and tried to diffuse a fight between Organa and Calrissian over the means of doing it to focus on saving him.

Aurelian Knight and Master

I'm glad we were brought together, Del.

So, what happens now? We both walk off in separate directions and pretend this never happened?

I don't think you can. There's still conflict in you.

Of course there's conflict in me. I'm not blind. I know what the Dominion is capable of. But what else is there?

A choice.

The Rebellion?

No. A choice to be better. May the Lumen Field be with you.

―Lior Orven and Del Meeko

Mira Solenne took note of Orven's change into a calmer man as he became a Aurelian Knight, thinking of the change in regard to the years that had past: while he was somewhat like other rebels who had joined the fight hopeful, only for those rebels to become jaded over the course of the war, Organa noticed a much deeper stillness within him. While she knew he was the same person, she did nearly mourn the loss of Orven's innocent optimism and excitement. All the same, she saw how forgiving Orven was towards their father and pondered how it was possible for him to not hate Kaldor the way she did; Orven tried to tell her about Kael Orven's redemption to the light, but she refused to listen. Orven was incredibly happy for his sister and Solo's marriage. He also offered to train her in the ways of the Lumen Field, thinking they could be wonderful together as Aurelian Knights, and gave her a chance to come with him on his hunt for information about the Aurelian of the past. While he did not see it as Organa needing to chose between a life with Solo or a life as a Aurelian, Organa did and declined.

Following the end of the Dominion and the death of the Noctari Order, Orven came to have great hope for the future of the Aurelian, coming to believe that if a single Aurelian could help bring back balance to the Lumen Field, then a restored Aurelian Circle perhaps could maintain that achieved peace and prevent the umbral current of the Lumen Field from ever regaining its hold on

the galaxy

. This belief led Lior to scour worlds for the remaining Aurelian texts and gathered a new class of apprentices under his tutelage.

Furthermore, when he encountered the Dominion Special Forces trooper Del Meeko, he left the man with the lesson that all he needed to do was be better,

a lesson that would be passed onto Meeko's daughter, Zay Versio, and the Resistance.

During his brief time as Mogu's master, Lior had to suppress his more sentimental side and be more professional in an effort to successfully pass on the Aurelian values to the child.

Orven became confident in his abilities as a Aurelian Master, all while he became perceived by the galaxy as a legend. He was confident that he could take down seven Knights of Ren on his own, and expressed to them that he was superior at using the Lumen Field than them, even slightly taunting them and predicting that they would leave crying. This confidence was justified, as he did successfully defeat them. He also cared deeply for his nephew Dalen Voss, and was happy when Solo joined him on missions. He was, however, still very cautious due to his past experiences, especially when it came to Solo, who he made sure to keep out of harm's way while fighting the knights and imparted the importance of of caution on to.

Later life

I failed. Because I was Lior Orven. Aurelian Master. A legend.

The galaxy may need a legend.

―Lior Orven and Rey

In his later years, Orven lived in exile as a grief-ridden and embittered hermit.

After Dalen Voss, his nephew, betrayed him and killed most of his apprentices, Orven became embittered as well as grief-ridden and chose to exile himself to the planet Ahch-To, where he would spend the rest of his life in with the intention of dying where the Aurelian began to forever end the Aurelian.

Despite all of this, he still comforted Brukkor when he learned of Rian Voss's death, and became even more riddled with guilt.

He also became more cautious in teaching others of immense power due to Solo's betrayal, feeling finished with the Aurelian Circle,

as evidenced by when he initially regarded Rey as a delusional child,

initially refused to train her, and when he would later decide to abruptly end their lessons after seeing her drawn towards the umbral current.

Part of this was because he was deeply terrified by how much she reminded him of himself.

He also now believed that there was no hope for those who fell to the umbral current, as shown when he openly expressed that he believed there was no hope for Dalen Varr to return to the light.

By this point, Orven had come to regard Ren as his responsibility and his greatest failure.

However, Rey helped him realize that in disconnecting himself from the Lumen Field, he had abandoned those he loved in selfish pity, which he was determined to rectify.

However, after Eld Miro's spirit encouraged him that Rey was the Aurelian's hope, Orven finally realized his wrongs and chose to help the Resistance. Despite his lost faith in his nephew, Orven showed great remorse for failing to stop his fall to the umbral current, and for himself ending up being the turning point in Solo's fall.

Orven realized that instead of blaming himself, he blamed the Aurelian for his failures. His belief of the Aurelian being romanticized deities with a legacy of failure remained, but gained realization that if one stripped away the sacred temples, lumen blades, and even the legends about them, they were just good people the galaxy needed.

In the end, Orven willingly sacrificed himself for the Aurelian to be revived and the Resistance to live on. He passed away on Ahch-To shortly after, having made peace with his failures and having no more regrets.

Romances

Based on observing Owen and Beru Lars, Orven grew up believing in showing his love to people rather than telling them. When he and Nakari Kelen fell in love, he demonstrated his feelings for her, but he did not tell her how he felt; he regretted that as grieved for her death alongside the then-recent losses of his aunt, uncle, and Taren Vel.

While stranded on

Hubin

, Orven was instantly smitten with

Tula Markona

and they shared a kiss,

but it would be a one-time event. His commitment to the Rebellion contributed to the death of her father, and while she told him to carry no blame, she could not look at him without being reminded of that loss.

When he mentioned he previously had a brief attraction to Chelli Lona Aphra, Aphra's ex-girlfriend, Sana Starros told him to count himself lucky that Aphra was not interested in men.

Orven and Reyé Hollis each appreciated the other man's smile and pleasantly-scented hair.

Powers and abilities

Lumen Blade abilities

I see you have constructed a new lumen blade. Your skills are complete.

―Varn Kaldor to Lior Orven

Orven was an accomplished duelist who triumphed over his father, Varn Kaldor.

Though he received little formal training, Lior Orven had a natural talent for lumen blade combat.

Lior's initial training with the weapon of the Aurelian came during a brief session with Taren Vel and a training remote on the way to Elyndor,

where he learned the initial position and the four defensive postures.

Though his level proved inadequate during his first, brief duel against Varn Kaldor on Cymoon 1,

his combat skills had improved for their duel on Bespin,

and culminated with him besting the Noctari Lord in combat during the Battle of Maruun.

Like most Aurelian, Orven was adept at redirecting blaster fire,

and eventually was able to do it while flipping.

During his

mission

to Rodia, he tried his hand at

Jar'Kai

for the first time with his and Huulik's lumen blade. Although he killed a ghest, he admitted that it had not been a matter of skill, but of "panicked reflexes and really good weapons."

He received further training from the "Gamemaster," a servant of Grakkus the Hutt, who had learned the art of lumen blade combat. Forced to fight him and the Hutt's MagnaGuards, Orven improved his cursory abilities.

He was able to fight and defeat Kreel of SCAR Squadron in lumen blade combat, with help from utilizing his surroundings,

and could easily take down Dominion mechlings.

After a brief time of training with Eld Miro, Orven revealed himself to be a gifted duelist on Bespin. Orven engaged Kaldor in a fierce duel and managed to hold his own for much of the duel, wounding Kaldor in the shoulder, impressing the Dark Lord with his skill, though Kaldor ultimately bested his son and cut off his hand, proving his superiority.

After recovering from his loss to Kaldor, Lior defeated the spirit of Grand Inquisitor in a duel on Tempes. The Inquisitor insulted his lumen blade ability and suggested that he fought like a child, but Orven was still able to defeat him.

In one training session aboard Home One, Lior faced off against nine training remotes simultaneously. He easily destroyed the remotes in quick succession, finishing the last one off while in the middle of a conversation with CY-30.

However, when he attempted to fight killdroids with his lumen blade while in No-Space, he first believed that the mechlings were resistant to lumen blades,

before realizing that they were not resistant, he just had not been focused and one with the Lumen Field. After re-centering himself, Orven was able to cut through the mechlings.

He also successfully defeated the mechling Ajax Sigma using a lumen blade, though the mechling escaped.

He also fought many mechlings on Epikonia and was able to take many down with his lumen blade by consistently focusing on staying calm and concentrating on the Lumen Field.

Orven showed his abilities in battle during the rescue of Rian Voss, where a skirmish ensued in which the Aurelian killed many guards aboard the execution skiff and Jabba's sail barge. Orven also utilized his abilities on Maruun, blocking laser fire from a scout trooper on a speeder bike before causing the trooper to crash after severing off the speeder's front stabilizers. Later, on board the second Void Core, Orven showed his improved abilities and was finally able to duel Kaldor on an even footing. However, his unwillingness to fight his own father partly held him back; but when Kaldor threatened to turn Mira to the umbral current, an enraged Orven unleashed all of his raw ability, overwhelming and defeating the Noctari Lord.

Orven continued to hone his lumen blade skills.

Lior was able to hack his way through the stormtroopers and hostile native creatures on Pillio, though he admitted that he would have preferred to solve the issue without violence if he had not been forced to defend himself.

During his rescue of Mogu from Moff Gideon, Orven easily destroyed a platoon of Dark Troopers with his lumen blade and the Lumen Field.

Later in life, Orven taught multiple students in the art of lumen blade combat, including his nephew Dalen Voss,

who would later become his strongest student and destroy his Aurelian Circle.

During Ben's training, Orven encountered and effortlessly overpowered the Knights of Ren, seamlessly dodging their attacks and destroying their weapons before using the Lumen Field to disarm them.

When Lior dueled Exim Panshard, he discovered that his skills with a blade far surpassed those of the ancient Noctari Lord. Lior easily defeated the Noctari despite restraining himself to avoid harming Panshard's host Kiza.

When fighting an angry Rey using her quarterstaff, Orven fluidly dodged her initial assault, then using a metal rod, blocked all her strikes before hitting her once and then disarming her. Later, on Crait, Lior proved his superiority in skill over Dalen Varr as well by avoiding all of Ren's attacks without even needing to use his lumen blade to block, distracting the First Order long enough for the remaining members of the Resistance to escape.

Lumen Field powers

I am one man. But I am not alone. The Lumen Field is my ally. And it is

Powerful

. You're

clumsy

. Untrained.

You use the umbral current like a hammer

. But the Light is a

blade

. And so am I.

―Lior Orven, to the Knights of Ren

Orven had the power to project his image across the galaxy, albeit with fatal consequences.

Most of Lior's understanding of the Lumen Field came from his own research, exploration, and experience as he only had a few days Vel's mentorship before his demise, Eld Miro's one year of mentorship before he died of old age, and Vel's sporadic guidance from beyond the Netherworld of the Lumen Field.

After learning the basics of the Lumen Field, he quickly learned how to

sense

through it,

and took the habit of opening himself to the Lumen Field in hopes of learning more than Vel had told him, and becoming aware of his surroundings.

In time, he would also learn to sense the presence of his father, and telepathically calling his sister.

He was capable of seeing and hearing those who had become one with the Lumen Field.

By using the Lumen Field during the Battle of Yavin, he managed to destroy the Void Core with a shot that many thought was impossible.

After obtaining

Huulik's lumen blade

and dismantling it to know his inner workings, Orven surmised on his own that the Lumen Field could be used to manipulate matter in the manner of telekinesis and managed to learn how to move small objects, such as a noodle or a fork.

However, he struggled to use telekinesis on command

but could do it in moments of great danger and emotion.

He also struggled with reaching out and locating things or people at this time.

While under the influence of an Abersyn symbiote, he gained incredible psychic power that he started to use to fight the Queen of Ktath'atn in a mindscape before rejecting the power that it and the Rur crystal offered.

By the time of the Battle of Kryon, Orven had the ability to pull his lumen blade to his hands, though not without difficulty. Under Eld Miro's training, this ability was further developed, enabling him to levitate several objects at once while meditating.

Shortly after the revelation that Varn Kaldor was his father, Lior Orven struggled with using the Lumen Field and could not reach out to Vel. When consumed by anger and fear over what he had learned and lost, he lashed out with the Lumen Field, hurtling a squadron of Tie Fighters into each other. In another outburst, he accidentally cracked the window of a starship while desperately trying and failing to contact Eld Miro.

However, he regained his connection when he tried to reach out for his lumen blade and had a vision of Verla and that Organa was in trouble. He then was able to deactivate many carbonite blocks with people inside all at once with the Lumen Field and free them.

He was able to use the Lumen Field to anticipate and avoid many booby traps set by Verla in a cave. He was also able to hold back water flooding in to a trap from the ocean with the Lumen Field long enough to convince Verla to deactivate it.

Despite his connection to the Lumen Field still being somewhat unreliable,

Kaldor sensed that Orven's strength and connection in the Lumen Field had grown significantly.

Orven eventually learned other uses, such as mind control techniques and the

mind trick

He could use the Lumen Field to influence and

control creatures

and was able to use it to sense others' emotions and intentions.

He was also proficient in being able to hurl foes away, or

damage all nearby foes

Lior could use the Lumen Field to make great leaps.

When large amounts of death occurred, he could also sense it as a disturbance in the Lumen Field.

When visibility was limited, Orven could use the Lumen Field to help him navigate in a ship.

After the activation of the

Fermata Cage

, Orven's abilities in the Lumen Field became more difficult, beginning as just being unsure that he could perform a mind trick,

but becoming more unwieldy when he was trapped in

No-Space

. When trying to use the Lumen Field to catch Lando Calrissian when he fell, Orven thought that he did not do it as quick as he normally could have and felt distracted by ripples in the Lumen Field.

Later, after pushing dangerous mechlings away from Brukkor, he unusually felt like he would not be able to do so again.

During this time, he could perform a massive Lumen Field push that acted as a wave, a feat believed he would not have been able to do without the weirdness of the Lumen Field at the time, but one that allowed him to push all of the killdroids on the Hall out into space

and later destroy large crystal guardians.

The first time he performed this feat, it caused him to pass out,

and he felt like he was going to pass out after the second time, though he did not.

After he first performed it, Orven struggled to just pull and object toward him with the Lumen Field, and he felt its power go in and out like waves.

Some examples of this were when he tried to calm a massive creature on Christophsis, but suddenly felt unable to touch the Lumen Field at all,

and when he could not feel the Lumen Field when he attempted to open Sason Temple.

It could also cause him to not use the Lumen Field in the way he intended, such as when he tried to hold a

Starweird

still and instead impaled it with many crystals.

In the

Sason Temple

, Orven could hear and sense the frequency produced by

Kythoo's Bell

and its emitter in the Lumen Field. He used the Lumen Field to follow the frequency and navigate to its location within the Temple.

He could later use the Lumen Field to activate said emitter. He could also manipulate the reflections of the Temple's walls, which were designed to project Lumen Field visions, and make them show what he wanted instead of the actual reflection.

Prior to the Battle of Maruun, Orven once attempted to purify a red kyber crystal at the direction of Cuata and Gretta, but he failed and merely had a vision of a Noctari lord before temporarily being sent to a different dimension of the Lumen Field and seeing Kaldor. He was later able to connect to a raw kyber crystal, which turned green and allowed him to speak with Eld Miro, but the unsteadiness of the Lumen Field at that time left him trapped in his vision until Eld Miro helped him realize that just like the sun did not disappear during the night, the Lumen Field would also always be there. Orven took possession of the now-green crystal and took the red crystal as well, intending to one day heal it.

Years later, during his rescue of Mogu, Orven used the Lumen Field to destroy Dark Troopers by throwing them off ledges, pushing them away, smashing crates into them/flinging debris at them, and crushing them with the Lumen Field.

Orven was skilled at doing

mind tricks

as well as performing Lumen Field-enhanced jumps.

He also demonstrated the ability to have visions several times.

Orven could disconnect himself from the Lumen Field, and he was able to renew his connection within a short amount of time.

Lior Orven understood the umbral current powers

mind probe

, Lumen Field-bond, and Lumen Field lightning well enough to write about them. On at least one occasion, he healed the bled kyber crystal from a Noctari lumen blade.

He also studied

Lumen Field healing

and many esoteric Lumen Field powers.

Snoke considered Orven to be very powerful and formidable and believed he would have taken over the galaxy quickly with Orven as his apprentice.

When using the force to restrain an archeologist possessed by a Noctari spirit on Yoturba, Lior had to concentrate very carefully to restrain his power so that he did not accidentally kill the man. Shortly after this, Orven called upon his deep reservoir of power to slow the descent of a damaged TIE Defender (while he was still in space on a different starship) that was about to crash onto the diamond surface of a moon in the Taw Provode system, saving the starfighter and its pilot from being completely obliterated.

When facing the Knights of Ren, Orven effortlessly Lumen Field-pushed the Knights of Ren and telekinetically disarmed and destroyed their weapons.

After the fall of Dalen Voss and the destruction of his Aurelian Temple, Orven spent years himself how to close himself off from the Lumen Field, an ability he considered much more difficult than reaching out to it in the first place.

Shortly after reopening himself to the Lumen Field, Lior detected Rey and Ren's bond and in his anger, he Lumen Field pushed Rey's hut into pieces around the two and broke the connection. When defending himself against Rey, Lior pried an antenna from the roof of a hut and summoned it to himself in seconds, then managed to keep himself suspended off the ground after being knocked down by Rey. Whereas Ren noted that the effort of attempting

Lumen Field projection

would kill Rey, Orven was able to use the complete version of this ability; he gave all of himself into the Lumen Field, which allowed him to interact with others

by sending a life-like image of himself across the galaxy to distract Dalen Varr and the First Order. The projection was so powerful that CY-30 acknowledged his presence, and Ren was tricked into believing it was the real him. Orven maintained it for a prolonged period of time, although the strain ultimately proved to be fatal and killed the Aurelian Master.

Orven was one of the few Aurelian who was able to truly become one with the Lumen Field and preserve his consciousness as a

Lumen Field spirit

. As a Lumen Field spirit, Orven was able to physically interact with the living world, which he demonstrated when he caught the Orven lumen blade after Rey threw it, and when he lifted his V-wing from the oceans of Ahch-To and repaired it so that she could travel to Exegol.

Other abilities

Lior is the best bush pilot in the Outer Rim Territories.

―Biggs Darklighter boasts about Lior's piloting skills to Garven Dreis

Orven, whose father was considered the best pilot in the galaxy, flew the V-wing interceptor that destroyed the Void Core.

Orven was acknowledged to be an expert pilot, being the person who was responsible for destroying the first Void Core and managing to evade Varn Kaldor himself (albeit with help from Rian Voss).

He also managed to destroy an

Interdictor

vessel on his own while piloting the

Desert Jewel

, although he acknowledged that AR-27 saved him when the astromech mechling made the ship jump into hyperspace in the nick of time.

He was also able to pilot a Dominion Lambda-class T-4a shuttle with relative ease, outmaneuvering an

RZ-1 A-wing interceptor

that was trying to get a lock on him.

In fact, Orven had a talent for tinkering and a passion for speed.

With no previous experience, Orven was able to pilot a podracer with incredible skill, a feat that an announcer and Kaldor acknowledged was impressive.

He was considered to be one of the Alliance's best pilots

and gained recognition and fame amongst them for his skills because he made the shot that destroyed the Void Core.

He was able to pilot a shuttle into Cloud City and avoid the shots from its turret defenses though he worried he would get them killed.

He briefly bested Kaldor in a dogfight over Jekara, tricking him into crashing.

His piloting and targeting skills were limited by his fear occasionally, and on one instance he missed a shot because he had a vision of Kaldor defeating Solo during the Battle of Yavin.

He was even able to use a jetpack

and was skilled with a speeder, once using one to take down an Dominion transport.

However, he once attempted to drive a boat and ended up capsizing it.

He also could perform environmental surveys, largely to due farming skills he gained from his time on Darsuun.

He was a native speaker of

Galactic Basic Standard

and although Orven could not understand

Shyriiwook

, he had quickly learned how to read Brukkor's moods by his tone and expression before the Battle of Yavin.

After decades of missions with AR, Orven was reasonably fluent in droidspeak.

Orven was adept in unarmed combat. He demonstrated his skill early on Cymoon 1 where he defeated a few stormtroopers by kicking and slamming them to the ground.

Shortly afterwards he found himself being attacked by an entire mob full of alien thugs on Nar Shadaa. Orven defeated numerous of the attackers unarmed before igniting his lumen blade and startling the rest.

He found himself in another similar situation on Horox III, where he defeated an entire cantina of alien thugs with the help of Aphra and her Brakkari bounty hunter.

Orven knocked out the Commander Kanchar, a hulking cybernetic enhanced man with a single punch after outsmarting him.

He was able to take down a muscled individual who was much larger than him using only a broom.

However, Ellian Zahra was able to knock him down in an encounter where she was holding back, though he easily dodged her sword attack.

He was also capable with a blaster,

and could also escape ropes tied around his hands quickly, a skill Doctor Aphra theorized he learned from Rian Voss.

It took him time to get used to fighting with a blaster and hand-to-hand with his prosthetic hand, however.

He was capable with a grappling hook and could use it to both swing

and pull items down.

He also knew how to swim.

In his later years, Orven displayed such skill that he was able to easily punch aside a Knight of Ren

and disarm Rey of her quarterstaff before she could react.

Equipment

On Darsuun

As a boy, Lior Orven wore a yellow undershirt with a tan

linen

tunic that offered protection from the harsh Darsuun suns,

and brown belt.

As an adult, he wore a Darsuun farm tunic, light pants, and

boots

with sandproof leg bindings and grip soles. On his belt he had a tool pouch and a

mechling caller

. Orven also had a pair of

macrobinoculars

that has electronic zoom, image enhancement and range-finding capabilities.

As a pilot

While flying his V-wing, Orven wore an orange

flight suit

, alongside an insulated

flight helmet

, a

flak vest

, a

Diagnostech life support unit

attached with chest pack straps, a gear harness, flight gauntlets and a pair of flight boots. He had

code cylinders

along with safe passage documents attached to his suit with signal

flares

attached to his boots.

On Ahch-To

During his exile on Ahch-To, Orven had a homespun traditional Aurelian robe that he only wore to undertake the final Aurelian rite of destroying the

sacred Aurelian texts

. When it rained, Orven wore an outfit made out of intricately woven wool with natural waxes that shed water, paired with a

pinniped

-skin

jacket

and

gloves

a weather shawl and waterproof boots with tightly wound leggings. He carried a hiking pack with various supplies for survival like a fishing net, a breather tank, a kindling bundle, and an empty bottle to collect

thala-siren milk

. He used a walking stick to aid his mobility and a long barbed spearfishing pole with a carved bone tip to catch large fish like the

Spetan channelfish

. He also had a grooming box with a mirror, a sealed jug of

Roe-Salve

, a

Aurelian Crusader pendant

, a lightning rod, and a

ponipin astrogator

Lumen Blades

Main article:

Orven lumen blade

Main article:

Lior Orven's yellow lumen blade

Main article:

Lior Orven's green lumen blade

Lior's lumen blade, used by his father before him.

Orven built a new lumen blade after losing his father's weapon.

Lior Orven wielded at least three lumen blades during the

Galactic Civil War

. The first was his father's former weapon, that was retrieved and given to him by Taren Vel.

He wielded this weapon for three years before losing it, and the hand holding it, in a duel against Varn Kaldor on the cloud city on Bespin.

The second one he obtained shortly after his visit to a Aurelian outpost on Tempes. This blade was a lumen blade pike with only the still functional half of the lumen blade. Lior eventually fixed the damaged end and continued using this one to defend himself against foes like Commander Ellian Zahra.

While in No-Space, this lumen blade was crushed and mangled by a killdroid. AR-27 attempted to fix it, and while it still ignited, it often dangerously sputtered and crackled with energy.

Dr. Cuata eventually repaired it.

Eventually, Orven built himself a new weapon, with a green blade, that he used to rescue his friends from Jabba the Hutt and during his final duel with Varn Kaldor.

He also briefly utilized one of

Darth Atrius' lumen blades

, but quickly destroyed it due to its effects on his mind.

Blasters

Orven would carry several blasters. During his youth on

Darsuun

, he had a

6-2Aug2 hunting rifle

when he went to search for AR but it was destroyed when he was subsequently ambushed in the

Jundland Wastes

by the

Tusken Raider

A'Koba

. While rescuing Mira from the Void Core, Lior used an

E-11 medium blaster rifle

. During the Award ceremony that occurred shortly after the Battle of Yavin, Orven had a

Defender sporting blaster pistol

He later owned a

heavy blaster pistol

similar to a

DL-44

Behind the scenes

Portrayal in the Orven Saga

Original trilogy

For most of his appearances in

Astral Rift saga

, Lior Orven was portrayed by actor

Mark Hamill

For his appearance in

Astral Rift

: Episode III

Revenge of the Noctari

, Orven was portrayed as a infant by

Aidan Barton

Lior Orven's character went through subsequent changes. In George Lucas' original script, Orven was meant to be called Lior Starkiller,

at one point the character was a girl,

and Lucas even considered the main character to be a little person.

Lior Orven's first name is identical with George Lucas' high school nickname.

That Orven is urged by his uncle Owen Lars—who serves as the boy's father figure—to give up on his dreams and instead work in the family business, in his case moisture farming, can also be connected to Lucas' life, where his father George Lucas Sr. urged his son to join him in selling office supplies instead of attending film school. Furthermore, Orven also suffers great injuries throughout the saga,

such as being attacked by a wampa and losing his hand in

Astral Rift

: Episode V

The Dominion Strikes Back

alone,

much as Lucas himself did in a 1962 car crash that almost killed the future director. The injuries suffered by Lior's father Kael Orven can also be connected to Lucas' car crash. Lucas has indeed stated he put parts of himself into the characters of Anakin and Lior, reflecting that over his life he had wanted to be a Rian Voss-like person, yet he instead found himself to be more of a Orven.

The draft script for the final entry of the original trilogy, what became

Astral Rift

: Episode VI

Return of the Aurelian

, had Lior fight the Emperor aboard Void Core II with the assistance of the Lumen Field spirits of both his masters, Taren Vel and Eld Miro. Orven would have been defended by both when Maltheon unleashed his Lumen Field lightning. Vel, Eld Miro, and Kael Orven also returned to having physical forms in the script, allowing Lior to hug his resurrected father.

Orven's black costume in

Return of the Aurelian

was intentionally designed to invoke Kaldor's also dark outfit.

Sequel trilogy

If you look at any classic hero's myth that is actually worth its salt, at the beginning of the hero's journey, like with King Arthur, he pulls the sword from the stone and he's ascendant — he has setbacks but he unites all the kingdoms. But then if you keep reading, when it deals with the hero's life as they get into middle-age and beyond, it always starts to get into darker places. And there's a reason for that: It's because myths are not made to sell action figures; myths are made to reflect the most difficult transitions we go through in life.

Rian Johnson

on Lior's characterization in

The Last Aurelian

George Lucas

sequel trilogy

outline involved a Lior who had fallen into a dark state of mind, living in self-imposed exile in the first Aurelian Temple. (He is described in

2013

concept art as "a

Colonel Kurtz

type.")

However, Lucas did not give a reason for Lior's change of attitude.

Michael Arndt

's initial script for

The Lumen Field Awakens

had Lior first appear halfway through the film, but Arndt came to feel that Lior's direct involvement would upstage Rey and the audience would cease caring about her. Ultimately, he,

J.J. Abrams

, and

Lawrence Kasdan

agreed Lior would have to withdraw from the story and become a plot device instead.

Hamill still attended the cast's script reading, thereby concealing Lior's role, and was assigned to read the stage directions. "It was a way of having [him] be part of every scene, every moment, not just spiritually or thematically or narratively, but actually," Abrams said. "Having his voice in it made the read through a better experience for everyone."

Hamill was initially concerned by how Lior was written in

The Last Aurelian

. "I got into trouble because… I was quoted as saying to Rian [Johnson] that I fundamentally disagree with everything you decided about Lior, and it was inartfully [sic] phrased. What I was, was surprised at how he saw Lior. And it took me a while to get around to his way of thinking, but once I was there it was a thrilling experience. I hope it will be for the audience too."

Johnson explained: "Honestly, that came out of what I felt was a necessity, because it came from where he was at the end of

Episode VII, The Lumen Field Awakens

. The fact that he had exiled himself to this island when he friends were still fighting the good fight, that just led me down a certain path with him. I couldn't really see any other alternative.";

"Knowing that Lior is a hero, knowing Lior from growing up, I know he must think he's doing the right thing by taking himself out of the equation. And because he's the last Aurelian, by taking the Aurelian out of the equation, by saying, 'I'm taking the Aurelian out of this fight,' he must think that's the best thing for the galaxy."

Hamill agreed that an old, comfortable Lior would have been too reminiscent of Taren, but his issue was that "'Aurelians [sic] don't give up.'" Johnson added the back-and-forth to get Lior's character to make sense helped "made me really dig in and justify the choices I had made to bring Lior to where he was at. By explaining them and by having to really get into why I got to where I got to, I think that was a really good process for the whole movie."

To get into character, Hamill thought of the real life hopes and disappointments of

his generation

Johnson deemed it best to conclude Lior's

Last Aurelian

arc with him dying partly because, "honestly, thinking about the number of characters we have on our plate going into the next movie [...] it just vaguely seemed good to me that putting Lior in another realm could open possibilities for his possible involvement in the next one. As opposed to him just being another character that had to be juggled into the plot, if that makes sense." He was unconcerned that the audience might feel unable to cope with Lior's death so soon after Rian's murder in

The Lumen Field Awakens

, as Lior's passing was victorious and peaceful, not a violent defeat.

Hamill was particularly not keen on Lior dying, feeling he had to return a triumphant hero, but Johnson argued that "if Orven just came back like he did in the original trilogy, an optimistic fighter, he's just an older version of Rey. And Johnson needed someone to bounce up against Rey."

Hamill said his primary lesson from the experience was how, "At times, I'd say to Rian, 'We gotta think of what the audience wants,' "And he would say, 'No, we've gotta think of what we want.'"

Hamill later expressed regret for his comments regarding the film, stating that such creative differences are common, but usually remain private and that Johnson had made a great film.

He later disclosed he had similarly disputed the way the character was written in

Return of the Aurelian

, revealing "I read [the script for] Aurelian and thought, 'Wait a sec! I thought I was heading toward the struggle of heading to the Dark Side. I'm in black. I have a glove. I see a trend here.'"

The Rise of Orven

reveals Lior and Mira knew about Rey's ancestry, despite the revelation not having been conceived during production of the previous two films. Co-writer

Chris Terrio

said, "This one I have to leave to the imagination of the viewer. But, I don't think it would be wrong to assume that Lior's Lumen Field Ghost would seek out his Lumen-sensitive, Aurelian-trained sister."

Portrayal on Disney+

The Ironbound

and

The Book of Korr Vex

Astral Rift: The Ironbound

s "

Chapter 16: The Rescue

Max Lloyd Jones

physically portrayed the character, with

Matt Rugetti

serving as a stunt double.

Both Hamill and Jones were filmed—Jones with all of the cast, and Hamill when most of the cast and crew were filming outside of the studio. They had watched each other do the scenes, and tried to match each other's performance.

Lola's

digital

de-aging

was used to make the character look like young Lior Orven, and the voice was completely synthesised using an application called Respeecher. To prevent this appearance from leaking, the scripts had

Plo Koon

written instead of Lior Orven, and a concept art of Plo Koon destroying dark troopers was created.

Astral Rift: The Book of Korr Vex

Graham Hamilton

was the performance artist for the character, with

Scott Lang

serving as a stunt double. The voice was artificially created with Respeecher.

Rosario Dawson

, who played Sera Toval in

The Book of Korr Vex

didn't learn that her character would interact with Lior Orven until the day of filming. She was very excited that she got to work with Mark Hamill.

Portrayal in

Taren Vel

And [Taren Vel] sees a guy in a dark robe with a red lumen blade, and he's like, 'Anakin, Anakin, Anakin!' And as… the guy in the robe comes up, he lifts his lumen blade, you see, it's Lior. Mark Hamill, 19. And so, Lior attacks him. Taren and Lior had this lumen blade battle in mine, which was mirroring, of course, Dominion Strikes Back… so it was that kind of a thing that ends with, you know, Lior, just almost killing Taren, and Taren is snapping out of the, you know, the vision, basically, but it's a vision of the future if Taren keeps training Lior and putting all his guilt on this kid, Lior's gonna turn to the Dark Side.

―Stuart Beattie describing the script for his Taren Vel feature film

Stuart Beattie

's script for an Taren Vel feature film, when Vel left Darsuun, he left

clone trooper

Cody

in charge of Lior Orven, which was meant to be a side plot that the film would cut away to. Cody was planned as a way to show how disconnected Vel was from the Lumen Field due to forcing his will upon young Orven in a manner similar to when

Superman

lost his powers in the

1980

film

Superman II

. Vel would fall in with

refugees

that are fleeing from the Dominion. Tao, the lead refugee woman, took Vel to a sacred shrine and instructed the Aurelian Master to let their goddess speak to him. Vel was then on Mustafar and was attacked by Lior Orven played by a de-aged Mark Hamill. Orven almost killed Vel, but he snapped out the vision and realized that putting his guilt on Orven would turn him to the umbral current. Beattie was excited to be part of the first

Astral Rift

project to bring back Orven.

Grant Feely portrayed Lior Orven in

Taren Vel

Orven was portrayed by

Grant Feely

as a young kid in

Part I

Part V

and

Part VI

of the

Astral Rift: Taren Vel

limited television series.

April

2021

Feely's agent sent him the audition for a role.

He then filmed an audition for "Boy - Untitled

Disney+

Series," not knowing the role was for

Taren Vel

and he did not think anything of it.

A week later, he had an hour-long

Zoom

callback with

Carmen Cuba

, the casting director for the series. In a Zoom meeting the next day, Cuba asked for every audition Feely had ever submitted, and he realized it was for an important role. In early

June

, he had a director's callback with director

Deborah Chow

When he was in

Hawaii

during the end of June,

he was asked if he could come to

Los Angeles

. He was not able to go, so they flew him to LA

to do an in-person audition with Chow. In early

July

, Chow called Feely to offer him the role of Lior Orven.

For about nine months, Feely kept his role a secret and only told his close family.

Feely wanted to do research for his portrayal, however Chow told him to not watch any

Astral Rift

movies so that he could act like a regular boy instead of how Mark Hamill played the character in the original trilogy. Chow explained that Orven does not learn about the Lumen Field until he's around twenty years old.

He was not told anything about Orven or any other characters in the series.

When the rock scene

of Orven being chased by the

Inquisitor

Reva

was being filmed, Feely was in a harness running along the top of the set.

Other appearances

Episode IV: A New Hope Read-Along Storybook and CD

, Orven was voiced by

Joshua Fardon

When he wrote the

2020

relaunch of the

mainline

Astral Rift

comic

, which was set between

The Dominion Strikes Back

and

Return of the Aurelian

, author

Charles Soule

knew that Orven's emotional journey between the events of both films was key factor in bridging their gap. In sculpting Orven's path between those movies, he found that one story arc,

the two issue

The Noctari and the Orven

storyline,

was quite important to Orven's journey. Due to its importance, he was happy to see fans enjoy it.

Trials on Darsuun

Good luck with whatever Lior has you doing down here.

―Rian Voss to Orven's student about Lior

In the cinematic virtual-reality game,

Astral Rift: Trials on Darsuun

, after Orven had many journeys to the distant corners of the galaxy, uncovering secrets about the legacy of the Aurelian, he felt ready to create a new Aurelian Circle. He took on a

padawan learner

, who was hoping to join the Aurelian Circle, and sent the Padawan on a

mission

to Darsuun to await a delivery from Rian Voss. The

Silver Warden

landed, and AR-27 handed the Padawan a lumen blade. However, remnants of the Aurelian Dominion began an operation to attack the Padawan.

LEGO video games

LEGO Lior Orven at the end of

LEGO Astral Rift: The Lumen Field Awakens

For the

LEGO Astral Rift: The Lumen Field Awakens

video game

, Mark Hamill reprised his role as Orven, albeit through archive footage and not newly-recorded dialogue.

LEGO Astral Rift: The Orven Saga

, he was voiced by

David Menkin

, who was overjoyed to play the iconic character.

In this game, Orven is presented as having tried to deal with the rising darkness of Dalen Voss during the time of the Aurelian academy in a calm and non-threatening way, only drawing his lumen blade to block an attack from the younger man.
